    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: I guess the main reason I came in is that I can’t seem to keep my head clear lately, especially at work. I’ll be sitting with a client and I realize I’ve lost the thread of what they’re saying, like I’m hearing the words but not actually holding onto them. Then afterward, I’ll try to write or review a chart note and I’ll end up rereading the same sentence over and over, and it still doesn’t really sink in.\n\nIt’s been embarrassing, honestly, because these are things I’m supposed to be able to do. Even simple decisions I used to make without thinking—like how to respond, what to document next, what the next step should be—I just kind of freeze. My mind feels slow and foggy, and I’m worried it’s starting to affect how I do my job.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this foggy feeling and difficulty concentrating?\n\nPatient: It’s been about a month, I think. It kind of started after the clinic lost some staff and my schedule got shifted around. I ended up taking more late-evening coverage, and there’s been a lot more crisis paperwork than usual.\n\nAt first I thought it was just being thrown off by the change, like maybe I was tired from the later hours or just adjusting, but it hasn’t really cleared up. It’s been pretty steady since then, and I keep noticing it during the day, especially when I’m trying to keep up with notes or stay focused with clients.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “sad” exactly. I mean… maybe sometimes, but it’s not like I’m crying all the time or anything clear like that. It’s more that I feel worn down and kind of flat, I guess. Like things take more effort than they should, and I don’t feel very much like myself.\n\nBut I’m not totally sure how to describe it. When you ask “down,” I think… yeah, maybe, in the sense that I feel heavier than usual and less able to bounce back. But I’ve mostly been focused on the foggy feeling and how it’s messing with work, so I haven’t really stopped to put a name to the mood part.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess not, not really. That’s actually been kind of noticeable now that you ask.\n\nLike, my guitar has just been sitting in the corner of my room. I used to pick it up without even thinking, just to mess around or play something familiar, but lately I’ll look at it and it feels like… I don’t know, like too much effort, or like I can’t imagine getting anything out of it. Same with soccer. I keep telling people I’m busy or tired, but honestly I’ve been bailing on it more than I’d like to admit.\n\nAnd I volunteer at the shelter sometimes, or I did, and I’ve been avoiding that too. It’s not that I suddenly don’t care about it, because I do, at least in my head. It’s just the actual going there and doing it feels weirdly pointless or heavy.\n\nEven smaller things, like cooking something decent or going hiking, which normally help me feel reset, haven’t really been doing that. If I try, it’s more like I’m just going through the motions because I know I’m supposed to enjoy it, but I don’t really feel much from it. It kind of makes everything feel flat.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not much comes to mind. I keep trying to think of something that still feels good in the way it used to, but it’s mostly like I’m doing things out of habit or because I know they’re supposed to be good for me.\n\nLike my guitar is still just sitting there in the corner, and I’ll notice it and think, “I should play for a few minutes,” but then I just… don’t. It doesn’t have that pull anymore. Soccer used to be something I’d look forward to during the week, and now I keep bailing or coming up with reasons not to go. Same with the shelter—I used to feel like that mattered and gave me something, but lately I’ve been avoiding it.\n\nEven stuff that’s easier, like cooking something decent or going hiking, doesn’t really land. I can do it sometimes, but it feels more like going through the motions than actually enjoying it. I’m trying to think of something I genuinely look forward to, and I don’t really have an answer, which feels kind of awful to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Yeah… appetite, definitely. Sleep I’m not really sure how to answer right now, but with eating, it’s been off.\n\nMost days I don’t really feel hungry in the normal way. Like it won’t occur to me that I haven’t eaten until someone says something, or until it’s suddenly late and I realize I skipped a meal without meaning to. It’s not like I’m trying to not eat or anything. It’s more that food just doesn’t register unless it’s put in front of me or someone reminds me.\n\nAnd I’ve noticed my pants have been getting loose, which was kind of the thing that made me go, “Okay, maybe this is actually happening.” Because at first I was telling myself I was just busy or distracted, but it’s been most days, not just once or twice. I’ll look back and realize I only had, like, something small, or I pushed off eating until it didn’t feel worth dealing with. It’s weird because I don’t feel actively against food, I just don’t get that pull to eat unless someone prompts me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I haven’t made a plan or anything like that. I don’t have some specific way I’m thinking of hurting myself, and I’m not, like, preparing to do that.\n\nBut I have been having these thoughts that scare me. It’s more like… I’ll be lying there or just sitting somewhere, and this thought comes up like, “It would be easier if I just didn’t wake up,” or “I don’t really want to have to keep doing this.” And then I get scared because I know that’s not a normal place for my head to go, or at least it feels like a bad sign.\n\nI don’t think I want to die exactly, but sometimes the idea of not having to deal with anything feels… relieving, I guess, which is hard to say out loud. And then I feel guilty or freaked out because I know what that can mean. So, no plan, but yeah, those thoughts have been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot, actually. I don’t always call it “worthless” in my head at first, but that’s basically where it goes.\n\nLike at work, if I miss one small detail in a note, or I realize I forgot to return a call right away, I don’t just think, “Okay, fix it.” I go straight to, “What is wrong with me? I’m useless. I’m letting the clients down, I’m letting everyone at the clinic down.” And it can be something that other people probably wouldn’t even see as a huge deal, but in my head it turns into this whole thing where I feel like I shouldn’t be trusted with anything.\n\nI’ll replay it over and over, too. Like, “How did I miss that? Why can’t I just be better? Everyone else is probably carrying me.” It feels really disproportionate when I say it out loud, but in the moment it feels completely true. And then I feel guilty on top of it, because these are clients who need help, and I start thinking I’m failing them by not being perfect with every little thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant changes in your energy levels?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my energy is pretty bad. It’s not just being tired like I stayed up too late or something. It feels heavier than that.\n\nEven really small stuff feels like it takes this huge amount of effort. Like showering—I’ll know I need to do it, and it’s not complicated, obviously, but it feels like I’m standing at the bottom of a hill just trying to talk myself into starting. Driving to the clinic feels the same way sometimes, like I’m already worn out before I even get there. And then once I’m there, finishing paperwork can feel almost impossible, even if it’s not that much. I’ll just sit there staring at it, feeling like I’m dragging around a backpack full of bricks.\n\nIt’s frustrating because these are normal things. They shouldn’t feel that hard. But lately everything feels like it costs more energy than I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I haven’t really noticed myself moving slower in a way where I can say, “Yes, definitely.” Like, nobody has told me I’m talking slower or walking differently or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I feel slow internally, if that makes sense—like it takes me longer to get myself going or to answer things—but I don’t know if that actually shows on the outside. I don’t think I’m speaking dramatically slower than usual. Maybe I pause more because I’m trying to think, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I haven’t clearly noticed it, at least not in a way I’m confident about.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I don’t think so in a clear way. It’s hard for me to tell because I feel slowed down on the inside a lot, like it takes effort to get myself to start something, but as far as actually moving more slowly—walking slower, getting dressed slower, people noticing I’m sluggish—I haven’t really had anyone point that out.\n\nMaybe I do sometimes take longer to do things, but I don’t know if that’s because I’m physically moving slower or because I’m just pausing and getting stuck before I start. So… I guess I can’t confidently say yes. I haven’t noticed it in a definite way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed myself being especially fidgety, like tapping my leg constantly or pacing around in a way that stands out.\n\nI do shift around sometimes, but I think everybody does that a little, and I haven’t had this feeling like I can’t sit still or I need to keep moving. If anything, when I’m sitting somewhere I’m more just kind of… stuck there, I guess. But in terms of restlessness specifically, I don’t think I’ve noticed that clearly.\n\nI might be missing it, though. Like, I don’t always pay close attention to what my body is doing unless someone points it out. But no, it’s not something I’d say has been obvious lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past month where you felt somewhat better, or were you feeling down or flat most of the time?\n\nPatient: It’s been mostly down or flat, honestly. I mean, there might be little moments where I’m distracted or not thinking about it as much, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a whole day where I felt clearly better.\n\nIt’s been going on for about a month now. I can kind of trace it back to when the clinic lost some staff and my schedule got shifted into more late-evening coverage, plus heavier crisis paperwork. Since then it’s just felt pretty constant, like the baseline has been lower most of the time rather than something that comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t weighed myself regularly, so I don’t know the exact number, but my pants have definitely gotten looser over the past month.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve been trying to lose weight or anything. I just don’t really feel hungry most days unless someone reminds me to eat, and then I realize I’ve skipped breakfast or lunch without meaning to. Sometimes I’ll get to late in the day and think, “Oh, I barely ate today.” It’s been happening enough that I noticed my clothes fitting differently.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of being down or flat have been present every day over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much every day. I mean, I don’t want to say every single minute of every day, because there are moments where I’m distracted or just going through the motions, but if you mean whether it’s been there as the overall feeling each day, then yes.\n\nIt’s been about a month now, I think. It seemed to start around when the clinic lost some staff and my schedule got pushed more into late-evening coverage, plus the crisis paperwork got heavier. Since then it’s been like… the day starts already kind of flat, and even if I’m busy, that feeling is still sort of underneath everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt completely back to your normal self?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had a day like that in the past two weeks. Not a full day where I felt like, “Okay, I’m back to myself.”\n\nIt’s been more like this ongoing thing for about a month now. I keep thinking back to when the clinic lost staff and my schedule got pushed into more late-evening coverage, and the crisis paperwork got heavier. Since then, it’s just felt like my normal baseline shifted downward. There might be brief stretches where I’m occupied or I’m not actively thinking about it, but I wouldn’t call that being back to normal. It’s not like I wake up one day and feel like myself again.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. Even simple decisions I used to make without thinking—like how to respond, what to document next, what the next step should be—I just kind of freeze. My mind feels slow and foggy, and I’m worried it’s starting to affect how I do my job.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing this foggy feeling and difficulty concentrating?\n\nPatient: It’s been about a month, I think. It kind of started after the clinic lost some staff and my schedule got shifted around. I ended up taking more late-evening coverage, and there’s been a lot more crisis paperwork than usual.\n\nAt first I thought it was just being thrown off by the change, like maybe I was tired from the later hours or just adjusting, but it hasn’t really cleared up. It’s been pretty steady since then, and I keep noticing it during the day, especially when I’m trying to keep up with notes or stay focused with clients.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say “sad” exactly. I mean… maybe sometimes, but it’s not like I’m crying all the time or anything clear like that. It’s more that I feel worn down and kind of flat, I guess. Like things take more effort than they should, and I don’t feel very much like myself.\n\nBut I’m not totally sure how to describe it. When you ask “down,” I think… yeah, maybe, in the sense that I feel heavier than usual and less able to bounce back. But I’ve mostly been focused on the foggy feeling and how it’s messing with work, so I haven’t really stopped to put a name to the mood part.\n\nDoctor: Have you been enjoying things you normally like to do?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I guess not, not really. That’s actually been kind of noticeable now that you ask.\n\nLike, my guitar has just been sitting in the corner of my room. I used to pick it up without even thinking, just to mess around or play something familiar, but lately I’ll look at it and it feels like… I don’t know, like too much effort, or like I can’t imagine getting anything out of it. Same with soccer. I keep telling people I’m busy or tired, but honestly I’ve been bailing on it more than I’d like to admit.\n\nAnd I volunteer at the shelter sometimes, or I did, and I’ve been avoiding that too. It’s not that I suddenly don’t care about it, because I do, at least in my head. It’s just the actual going there and doing it feels weirdly pointless or heavy.\n\nEven smaller things, like cooking something decent or going hiking, which normally help me feel reset, haven’t really been doing that. If I try, it’s more like I’m just going through the motions because I know I’m supposed to enjoy it, but I don’t really feel much from it. It kind of makes everything feel flat.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not much comes to mind. I keep trying to think of something that still feels good in the way it used to, but it’s mostly like I’m doing things out of habit or because I know they’re supposed to be good for me.\n\nLike my guitar is still just sitting there in the corner, and I’ll notice it and think, “I should play for a few minutes,” but then I just… don’t. It doesn’t have that pull anymore. Soccer used to be something I’d look forward to during the week, and now I keep bailing or coming up with reasons not to go. Same with the shelter—I used to feel like that mattered and gave me something, but lately I’ve been avoiding it.\n\nEven stuff that’s easier, like cooking something decent or going hiking, doesn’t really land. I can do it sometimes, but it feels more like going through the motions than actually enjoying it. I’m trying to think of something I genuinely look forward to, and I don’t really have an answer, which feels kind of awful to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Yeah… appetite, definitely. Sleep I’m not really sure how to answer right now, but with eating, it’s been off.\n\nMost days I don’t really feel hungry in the normal way. Like it won’t occur to me that I haven’t eaten until someone says something, or until it’s suddenly late and I realize I skipped a meal without meaning to. It’s not like I’m trying to not eat or anything. It’s more that food just doesn’t register unless it’s put in front of me or someone reminds me.\n\nAnd I’ve noticed my pants have been getting loose, which was kind of the thing that made me go, “Okay, maybe this is actually happening.” Because at first I was telling myself I was just busy or distracted, but it’s been most days, not just once or twice. I’ll look back and realize I only had, like, something small, or I pushed off eating until it didn’t feel worth dealing with. It’s weird because I don’t feel actively against food, I just don’t get that pull to eat unless someone prompts me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I haven’t made a plan or anything like that. I don’t have some specific way I’m thinking of hurting myself, and I’m not, like, preparing to do that.\n\nBut I have been having these thoughts that scare me. It’s more like… I’ll be lying there or just sitting somewhere, and this thought comes up like, “It would be easier if I just didn’t wake up,” or “I don’t really want to have to keep doing this.” And then I get scared because I know that’s not a normal place for my head to go, or at least it feels like a bad sign.\n\nI don’t think I want to die exactly, but sometimes the idea of not having to deal with anything feels… relieving, I guess, which is hard to say out loud. And then I feel guilty or freaked out because I know what that can mean. So, no plan, but yeah, those thoughts have been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot, actually. I don’t always call it “worthless” in my head at first, but that’s basically where it goes.\n\nLike at work, if I miss one small detail in a note, or I realize I forgot to return a call right away, I don’t just think, “Okay, fix it.” I go straight to, “What is wrong with me? I’m useless. I’m letting the clients down, I’m letting everyone at the clinic down.” And it can be something that other people probably wouldn’t even see as a huge deal, but in my head it turns into this whole thing where I feel like I shouldn’t be trusted with anything.\n\nI’ll replay it over and over, too. Like, “How did I miss that? Why can’t I just be better? Everyone else is probably carrying me.” It feels really disproportionate when I say it out loud, but in the moment it feels completely true. And then I feel guilty on top of it, because these are clients who need help, and I start thinking I’m failing them by not being perfect with every little thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant changes in your energy levels?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my energy is pretty bad. It’s not just being tired like I stayed up too late or something. It feels heavier than that.\n\nEven really small stuff feels like it takes this huge amount of effort. Like showering—I’ll know I need to do it, and it’s not complicated, obviously, but it feels like I’m standing at the bottom of a hill just trying to talk myself into starting. Driving to the clinic feels the same way sometimes, like I’m already worn out before I even get there. And then once I’m there, finishing paperwork can feel almost impossible, even if it’s not that much. I’ll just sit there staring at it, feeling like I’m dragging around a backpack full of bricks.\n\nIt’s frustrating because these are normal things. They shouldn’t feel that hard. But lately everything feels like it costs more energy than I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I haven’t really noticed myself moving slower in a way where I can say, “Yes, definitely.” Like, nobody has told me I’m talking slower or walking differently or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I feel slow internally, if that makes sense—like it takes me longer to get myself going or to answer things—but I don’t know if that actually shows on the outside. I don’t think I’m speaking dramatically slower than usual. Maybe I pause more because I’m trying to think, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I haven’t clearly noticed it, at least not in a way I’m confident about.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I don’t think so in a clear way. It’s hard for me to tell because I feel slowed down on the inside a lot, like it takes effort to get myself to start something, but as far as actually moving more slowly—walking slower, getting dressed slower, people noticing I’m sluggish—I haven’t really had anyone point that out.\n\nMaybe I do sometimes take longer to do things, but I don’t know if that’s because I’m physically moving slower or because I’m just pausing and getting stuck before I start. So… I guess I can’t confidently say yes. I haven’t noticed it in a definite way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed myself being especially fidgety, like tapping my leg constantly or pacing around in a way that stands out.\n\nI do shift around sometimes, but I think everybody does that a little, and I haven’t had this feeling like I can’t sit still or I need to keep moving. If anything, when I’m sitting somewhere I’m more just kind of… stuck there, I guess. Like, I don’t always pay close attention to what my body is doing unless someone points it out. I mean, there might be little moments where I’m distracted or not thinking about it as much, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a whole day where I felt clearly better.\n\nIt’s been going on for about a month now. I just don’t really feel hungry most days unless someone reminds me to eat, and then I realize I’ve skipped breakfast or lunch without meaning to. Sometimes I’ll get to late in the day and think, “Oh, I barely ate today.” It’s been happening enough that I noticed my clothes fitting differently.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of being down or flat have been present every day over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much every day. It seemed to start around when the clinic lost some staff and my schedule got pushed more into late-evening coverage, plus the crisis paperwork got heavier. Since then it’s been like… the day starts already kind of flat, and even if I’m busy, that feeling is still sort of underneath everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt completely back to your normal self?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had a day like that in the past two weeks. It’s not like I wake up one day and feel like myself again.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling an internal restlessness that makes you want to move around or engage in activities to relieve it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d describe it that way, no. I keep trying to think of something that still feels good in the way it used to, but it’s mostly like I’m doing things out of habit or because I know they’re supposed to be good for me.\n\nLike my guitar is still just sitting there in the corner, and I’ll notice it and think, “I should play for a few minutes,” but then I just… don’t. It doesn’t have that pull anymore. Soccer used to be something I’d look forward to during the week, and now I keep bailing or coming up with reasons not to go. Same with the shelter—I used to feel like that mattered and gave me something, but lately I’ve been avoiding it.\n\nEven stuff that’s easier, like cooking something decent or going hiking, doesn’t really land. I can do it sometimes, but it feels more like going through the motions than actually enjoying it. I’m trying to think of something I genuinely look forward to, and I don’t really have an answer, which feels kind of awful to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Yeah… appetite, definitely. Sleep I’m not really sure how to answer right now, but with eating, it’s been off.\n\nMost days I don’t really feel hungry in the normal way. Like it won’t occur to me that I haven’t eaten until someone says something, or until it’s suddenly late and I realize I skipped a meal without meaning to. It’s not like I’m trying to not eat or anything. It’s more that food just doesn’t register unless it’s put in front of me or someone reminds me.\n\nAnd I’ve noticed my pants have been getting loose, which was kind of the thing that made me go, “Okay, maybe this is actually happening.” Because at first I was telling myself I was just busy or distracted, but it’s been most days, not just once or twice. I’ll look back and realize I only had, like, something small, or I pushed off eating until it didn’t feel worth dealing with. It’s weird because I don’t feel actively against food, I just don’t get that pull to eat unless someone prompts me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I haven’t made a plan or anything like that. I don’t have some specific way I’m thinking of hurting myself, and I’m not, like, preparing to do that.\n\nBut I have been having these thoughts that scare me. It’s more like… I’ll be lying there or just sitting somewhere, and this thought comes up like, “It would be easier if I just didn’t wake up,” or “I don’t really want to have to keep doing this.” And then I get scared because I know that’s not a normal place for my head to go, or at least it feels like a bad sign.\n\nI don’t think I want to die exactly, but sometimes the idea of not having to deal with anything feels… relieving, I guess, which is hard to say out loud. And then I feel guilty or freaked out because I know what that can mean. So, no plan, but yeah, those thoughts have been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot, actually. I don’t always call it “worthless” in my head at first, but that’s basically where it goes.\n\nLike at work, if I miss one small detail in a note, or I realize I forgot to return a call right away, I don’t just think, “Okay, fix it.” I go straight to, “What is wrong with me? I’m useless. I’m letting the clients down, I’m letting everyone at the clinic down.” And it can be something that other people probably wouldn’t even see as a huge deal, but in my head it turns into this whole thing where I feel like I shouldn’t be trusted with anything.\n\nI’ll replay it over and over, too. Like, “How did I miss that? Why can’t I just be better? Everyone else is probably carrying me.” It feels really disproportionate when I say it out loud, but in the moment it feels completely true. And then I feel guilty on top of it, because these are clients who need help, and I start thinking I’m failing them by not being perfect with every little thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant changes in your energy levels?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my energy is pretty bad. It’s not just being tired like I stayed up too late or something. It feels heavier than that.\n\nEven really small stuff feels like it takes this huge amount of effort. Like showering—I’ll know I need to do it, and it’s not complicated, obviously, but it feels like I’m standing at the bottom of a hill just trying to talk myself into starting. Driving to the clinic feels the same way sometimes, like I’m already worn out before I even get there. And then once I’m there, finishing paperwork can feel almost impossible, even if it’s not that much. I’ll just sit there staring at it, feeling like I’m dragging around a backpack full of bricks.\n\nIt’s frustrating because these are normal things. They shouldn’t feel that hard. But lately everything feels like it costs more energy than I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I haven’t really noticed myself moving slower in a way where I can say, “Yes, definitely.” Like, nobody has told me I’m talking slower or walking differently or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I feel slow internally, if that makes sense—like it takes me longer to get myself going or to answer things—but I don’t know if that actually shows on the outside. I don’t think I’m speaking dramatically slower than usual. Maybe I pause more because I’m trying to think, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I haven’t clearly noticed it, at least not in a way I’m confident about.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I don’t think so in a clear way. It’s hard for me to tell because I feel slowed down on the inside a lot, like it takes effort to get myself to start something, but as far as actually moving more slowly—walking slower, getting dressed slower, people noticing I’m sluggish—I haven’t really had anyone point that out.\n\nMaybe I do sometimes take longer to do things, but I don’t know if that’s because I’m physically moving slower or because I’m just pausing and getting stuck before I start. So… I guess I can’t confidently say yes. I haven’t noticed it in a definite way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed myself being especially fidgety, like tapping my leg constantly or pacing around in a way that stands out.\n\nI do shift around sometimes, but I think everybody does that a little, and I haven’t had this feeling like I can’t sit still or I need to keep moving. If anything, when I’m sitting somewhere I’m more just kind of… stuck there, I guess. But in terms of restlessness specifically, I don’t think I’ve noticed that clearly.\n\nI might be missing it, though. Like, I don’t always pay close attention to what my body is doing unless someone points it out. But no, it’s not something I’d say has been obvious lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past month where you felt somewhat better, or were you feeling down or flat most of the time?\n\nPatient: It’s been mostly down or flat, honestly. I mean, there might be little moments where I’m distracted or not thinking about it as much, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a whole day where I felt clearly better.\n\nIt’s been going on for about a month now. I can kind of trace it back to when the clinic lost some staff and my schedule got shifted into more late-evening coverage, plus heavier crisis paperwork. Since then it’s just felt pretty constant, like the baseline has been lower most of the time rather than something that comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t weighed myself regularly, so I don’t know the exact number, but my pants have definitely gotten looser over the past month.\n\nIt’s not like I’ve been trying to lose weight or anything. I just don’t really feel hungry most days unless someone reminds me to eat, and then I realize I’ve skipped breakfast or lunch without meaning to. Sometimes I’ll get to late in the day and think, “Oh, I barely ate today.” It’s been happening enough that I noticed my clothes fitting differently.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of being down or flat have been present every day over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much every day. I mean, I don’t want to say every single minute of every day, because there are moments where I’m distracted or just going through the motions, but if you mean whether it’s been there as the overall feeling each day, then yes.\n\nIt’s been about a month now, I think. It seemed to start around when the clinic lost some staff and my schedule got pushed more into late-evening coverage, plus the crisis paperwork got heavier. Since then it’s been like… the day starts already kind of flat, and even if I’m busy, that feeling is still sort of underneath everything.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past two weeks where you felt completely back to your normal self?\n\nPatient: No, I don’t think I’ve had a day like that in the past two weeks. Not a full day where I felt like, “Okay, I’m back to myself.”\n\nIt’s been more like this ongoing thing for about a month now. I keep thinking back to when the clinic lost staff and my schedule got pushed into more late-evening coverage, and the crisis paperwork got heavier. Since then, it’s just felt like my normal baseline shifted downward. There might be brief stretches where I’m occupied or I’m not actively thinking about it, but I wouldn’t call that being back to normal. It’s not like I wake up one day and feel like myself again.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling an internal restlessness that makes you want to move around or engage in activities to relieve it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d describe it that way, no. Like, I don’t think I’ve had this feeling where I can’t sit still or I need to pace around to get relief.\n\nSometimes I shift around or feel uncomfortable, but it doesn’t feel like an internal motor or anything like that. It’s more like… I don’t know, I’m just sitting there. I haven’t noticed myself needing to move around or go do things because of restlessness.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to keep moving or doing something to calm down?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. I mean, I’m trying to think about it carefully because I do sometimes feel uncomfortable just sitting with myself, but I don’t think it’s that specific feeling of needing to keep moving to calm down.\n\nI’m not pacing around the room or feeling like I have to constantly be doing something with my hands or walking it off. If I’m sitting, I can stay sitting. It’s more just… I feel kind of stuck or low, not keyed up in that way. So I’d say no, I haven’t really noticed that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I’m useless. That’s actually been kind of noticeable now that you ask.\n\nLike, my guitar has just been sitting in the corner of my room. I used to pick it up without even thinking, just to mess around or play something familiar, but lately I’ll look at it and it feels like… I don’t know, like too much effort, or like I can’t imagine getting anything out of it. Same with soccer. I keep telling people I’m busy or tired, but honestly I’ve been bailing on it more than I’d like to admit.\n\nAnd I volunteer at the shelter sometimes, or I did, and I’ve been avoiding that too. It’s not that I suddenly don’t care about it, because I do, at least in my head. It’s just the actual going there and doing it feels weirdly pointless or heavy.\n\nEven smaller things, like cooking something decent or going hiking, which normally help me feel reset, haven’t really been doing that. If I try, it’s more like I’m just going through the motions because I know I’m supposed to enjoy it, but I don’t really feel much from it. It kind of makes everything feel flat.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities that you still find pleasure in or look forward to?\n\nPatient: Honestly… not much comes to mind. I keep trying to think of something that still feels good in the way it used to, but it’s mostly like I’m doing things out of habit or because I know they’re supposed to be good for me.\n\nLike my guitar is still just sitting there in the corner, and I’ll notice it and think, “I should play for a few minutes,” but then I just… don’t. It doesn’t have that pull anymore. Soccer used to be something I’d look forward to during the week, and now I keep bailing or coming up with reasons not to go. Same with the shelter—I used to feel like that mattered and gave me something, but lately I’ve been avoiding it.\n\nEven stuff that’s easier, like cooking something decent or going hiking, doesn’t really land. I can do it sometimes, but it feels more like going through the motions than actually enjoying it. I’m trying to think of something I genuinely look forward to, and I don’t really have an answer, which feels kind of awful to say.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any changes in your appetite or sleep patterns?\n\nPatient: Yeah… appetite, definitely. Sleep I’m not really sure how to answer right now, but with eating, it’s been off.\n\nMost days I don’t really feel hungry in the normal way. Like it won’t occur to me that I haven’t eaten until someone says something, or until it’s suddenly late and I realize I skipped a meal without meaning to. It’s not like I’m trying to not eat or anything. It’s more that food just doesn’t register unless it’s put in front of me or someone reminds me.\n\nAnd I’ve noticed my pants have been getting loose, which was kind of the thing that made me go, “Okay, maybe this is actually happening.” Because at first I was telling myself I was just busy or distracted, but it’s been most days, not just once or twice. I’ll look back and realize I only had, like, something small, or I pushed off eating until it didn’t feel worth dealing with. It’s weird because I don’t feel actively against food, I just don’t get that pull to eat unless someone prompts me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I haven’t made a plan or anything like that. I don’t have some specific way I’m thinking of hurting myself, and I’m not, like, preparing to do that.\n\nBut I have been having these thoughts that scare me. It’s more like… I’ll be lying there or just sitting somewhere, and this thought comes up like, “It would be easier if I just didn’t wake up,” or “I don’t really want to have to keep doing this.” And then I get scared because I know that’s not a normal place for my head to go, or at least it feels like a bad sign.\n\nI don’t think I want to die exactly, but sometimes the idea of not having to deal with anything feels… relieving, I guess, which is hard to say out loud. And then I feel guilty or freaked out because I know what that can mean. So, no plan, but yeah, those thoughts have been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot, actually. I don’t always call it “worthless” in my head at first, but that’s basically where it goes.\n\nLike at work, if I miss one small detail in a note, or I realize I forgot to return a call right away, I don’t just think, “Okay, fix it.” I go straight to, “What is wrong with me? I’m useless. I’m letting the clients down, I’m letting everyone at the clinic down.” And it can be something that other people probably wouldn’t even see as a huge deal, but in my head it turns into this whole thing where I feel like I shouldn’t be trusted with anything.\n\nI’ll replay it over and over, too. Like, “How did I miss that? Why can’t I just be better? Everyone else is probably carrying me.” It feels really disproportionate when I say it out loud, but in the moment it feels completely true. And then I feel guilty on top of it, because these are clients who need help, and I start thinking I’m failing them by not being perfect with every little thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant changes in your energy levels?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my energy is pretty bad. It’s not just being tired like I stayed up too late or something. It feels heavier than that.\n\nEven really small stuff feels like it takes this huge amount of effort. Like showering—I’ll know I need to do it, and it’s not complicated, obviously, but it feels like I’m standing at the bottom of a hill just trying to talk myself into starting. Driving to the clinic feels the same way sometimes, like I’m already worn out before I even get there. And then once I’m there, finishing paperwork can feel almost impossible, even if it’s not that much. I’ll just sit there staring at it, feeling like I’m dragging around a backpack full of bricks.\n\nIt’s frustrating because these are normal things. They shouldn’t feel that hard. But lately everything feels like it costs more energy than I have.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I haven’t really noticed myself moving slower in a way where I can say, “Yes, definitely.” Like, nobody has told me I’m talking slower or walking differently or anything like that.\n\nSometimes I feel slow internally, if that makes sense—like it takes me longer to get myself going or to answer things—but I don’t know if that actually shows on the outside. I don’t think I’m speaking dramatically slower than usual. Maybe I pause more because I’m trying to think, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I haven’t clearly noticed it, at least not in a way I’m confident about.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I don’t think so in a clear way. It’s hard for me to tell because I feel slowed down on the inside a lot, like it takes effort to get myself to start something, but as far as actually moving more slowly—walking slower, getting dressed slower, people noticing I’m sluggish—I haven’t really had anyone point that out.\n\nMaybe I do sometimes take longer to do things, but I don’t know if that’s because I’m physically moving slower or because I’m just pausing and getting stuck before I start. So… I guess I can’t confidently say yes. I haven’t noticed it in a definite way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed myself being especially fidgety, like tapping my leg constantly or pacing around in a way that stands out.\n\nI do shift around sometimes, but I think everybody does that a little, and I haven’t had this feeling like I can’t sit still or I need to keep moving. If anything, when I’m sitting somewhere I’m more just kind of… stuck there, I guess. But in terms of restlessness specifically, I don’t think I’ve noticed that clearly.\n\nI might be missing it, though. Like, I don’t always pay close attention to what my body is doing unless someone points it out. But no, it’s not something I’d say has been obvious lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past month where you felt somewhat better, or were you feeling down or flat most of the time?\n\nPatient: It’s been mostly down or flat, honestly. I mean, there might be little moments where I’m distracted or not thinking about it as much, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a whole day where I felt clearly better.\n\nIt’s been going on for about a month now. I can kind of trace it back to when the clinic lost some staff and my schedule got shifted into more late-evening coverage, plus heavier crisis paperwork. Since then it’s just felt pretty constant, like the baseline has been lower most of the time rather than something that comes and goes.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I just don’t really feel hungry most days unless someone reminds me to eat, and then I realize I’ve skipped breakfast or lunch without meaning to. Sometimes I’ll get to late in the day and think, “Oh, I barely ate today.” It’s been happening enough that I noticed my clothes fitting differently.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of being down or flat have been present every day over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much every day. I mean, I don’t want to say every single minute of every day, because there are moments where I’m distracted or just going through the motions, but if you mean whether it’s been there as the overall feeling each day, then yes.\n\nIt’s been about a month now, I think. There might be brief stretches where I’m occupied or I’m not actively thinking about it, but I wouldn’t call that being back to normal. It’s not like I wake up one day and feel like myself again.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling an internal restlessness that makes you want to move around or engage in activities to relieve it?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure I’d describe it that way, no. It’s more like… I don’t know, I’m just sitting there. I haven’t noticed myself needing to move around or go do things because of restlessness.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you need to keep moving or doing something to calm down?\n\nPatient: Not really, no. It’s not like I’m trying to not eat or anything. It’s more that food just doesn’t register unless it’s put in front of me or someone reminds me.\n\nAnd I’ve noticed my pants have been getting loose, which was kind of the thing that made me go, “Okay, maybe this is actually happening.” Because at first I was telling myself I was just busy or distracted, but it’s been most days, not just once or twice. I’ll look back and realize I only had, like, something small, or I pushed off eating until it didn’t feel worth dealing with. It’s weird because I don’t feel actively against food, I just don’t get that pull to eat unless someone prompts me.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I mean, I haven’t made a plan or anything like that. I don’t have some specific way I’m thinking of hurting myself, and I’m not, like, preparing to do that.\n\nBut I have been having these thoughts that scare me. It’s more like… I’ll be lying there or just sitting somewhere, and this thought comes up like, “It would be easier if I just didn’t wake up,” or “I don’t really want to have to keep doing this.” And then I get scared because I know that’s not a normal place for my head to go, or at least it feels like a bad sign.\n\nI don’t think I want to die exactly, but sometimes the idea of not having to deal with anything feels… relieving, I guess, which is hard to say out loud. And then I feel guilty or freaked out because I know what that can mean. So, no plan, but yeah, those thoughts have been there.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any feelings of worthlessness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… a lot, actually. I don’t always call it “worthless” in my head at first, but that’s basically where it goes.\n\nLike at work, if I miss one small detail in a note, or I realize I forgot to return a call right away, I don’t just think, “Okay, fix it.” I go straight to, “What is wrong with me? I’m useless. I’m letting the clients down, I’m letting everyone at the clinic down.” And it can be something that other people probably wouldn’t even see as a huge deal, but in my head it turns into this whole thing where I feel like I shouldn’t be trusted with anything.\n\nI’ll replay it over and over, too. Like, “How did I miss that? Why can’t I just be better? Everyone else is probably carrying me.” It feels really disproportionate when I say it out loud, but in the moment it feels completely true. And then I feel guilty on top of it, because these are clients who need help, and I start thinking I’m failing them by not being perfect with every little thing.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing any significant changes in your energy levels?\n\nPatient: Yeah… my energy is pretty bad. It’s not just being tired like I stayed up too late or something. It feels heavier than that.\n\nEven really small stuff feels like it takes this huge amount of effort. Like showering—I’ll know I need to do it, and it’s not complicated, obviously, but it feels like I’m standing at the bottom of a hill just trying to talk myself into starting. Driving to the clinic feels the same way sometimes, like I’m already worn out before I even get there. And then once I’m there, finishing paperwork can feel almost impossible, even if it’s not that much. I’ll just sit there staring at it, feeling like I’m dragging around a backpack full of bricks.\n\nIt’s frustrating because these are normal things. They shouldn’t feel that hard. I don’t think I’m speaking dramatically slower than usual. Maybe I pause more because I’m trying to think, but I don’t know if that’s what you mean.\n\nSo… I guess my answer is I haven’t clearly noticed it, at least not in a way I’m confident about.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: I don’t really know. I mean, I don’t think so in a clear way. It’s hard for me to tell because I feel slowed down on the inside a lot, like it takes effort to get myself to start something, but as far as actually moving more slowly—walking slower, getting dressed slower, people noticing I’m sluggish—I haven’t really had anyone point that out.\n\nMaybe I do sometimes take longer to do things, but I don’t know if that’s because I’m physically moving slower or because I’m just pausing and getting stuck before I start. So… I guess I can’t confidently say yes. I haven’t noticed it in a definite way.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling restless or fidgety lately?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I wouldn’t say I’ve noticed myself being especially fidgety, like tapping my leg constantly or pacing around in a way that stands out.\n\nI do shift around sometimes, but I think everybody does that a little, and I haven’t had this feeling like I can’t sit still or I need to keep moving. If anything, when I’m sitting somewhere I’m more just kind of… stuck there, I guess. But in terms of restlessness specifically, I don’t think I’ve noticed that clearly.\n\nI might be missing it, though. Like, I don’t always pay close attention to what my body is doing unless someone points it out. But no, it’s not something I’d say has been obvious lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any days in the past month where you felt somewhat better, or were you feeling down or flat most of the time?\n\nPatient: It’s been mostly down or flat, honestly. I mean, there might be little moments where I’m distracted or not thinking about it as much, but I wouldn’t say I’ve had a whole day where I felt clearly better.\n\nIt’s been going on for about a month now. I can kind of trace it back to when the clinic lost some staff and my schedule got shifted into more late-evening coverage, plus heavier crisis paperwork. Sometimes I’ll get to late in the day and think, “Oh, I barely ate today.” It’s been happening enough that I noticed my clothes fitting differently.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that these feelings of being down or flat have been present every day over the past month?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much every day. I mean, I don’t want to say every single minute of every day, because there are moments where I’m distracted or just going through the motions, but if you mean whether it’s been there as the overall feeling each day, then yes.\n\nIt’s been about a month now, I think.       "No mention of manic or hypomanic episodes",
```json
      "No indication of psychotic symptoms"
    ]
  }
}
```
